is the
reversed ‘dagger’ channel defined by
c^{{\dagger}}_{\omega}(y)=\omega\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$|$}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{$\scriptstyle c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg{\kern-4.52083pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 1.50694pt}\scriptstyle\gg{\kern-0.42192pt}={\kern 1.05486pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\gg=$}}}\mathbf{1}_{y}$}
. Abbreviating
\omega^{\prime}\coloneqq c^{{\dagger}}_{\omega}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$=\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$={\kern-6.45831pt}\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle={\kern-0.60275pt}\ll{\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle=\ll$}}}\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)
, we have associated validity
increases and divergence decreases:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathsl{mn}[K]\big(c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$=\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$={\kern-6.45831pt}\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle={\kern-0.60275pt}\ll{\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle=\ll$}}}\omega^{\prime}\big)(\psi)\hskip 2.0pt\,=\,\hskip 2.0ptc\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$=\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$={\kern-6.45831pt}\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle={\kern-0.60275pt}\ll{\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle=\ll$}}}\omega^{\prime}\mathbin{\smash{\models_{\hskip-3.61664pt{\raisebox{-1.20552pt}{\tiny J}}}}\,}\psi&\,\geq&c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$=\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$={\kern-6.45831pt}\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle={\kern-0.60275pt}\ll{\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle=\ll$}}}\omega\mathbin{\smash{\models_{\hskip-3.61664pt{\raisebox{-1.20552pt}{\tiny J}}}}\,}\psi\hskip 2.0pt\,=\,\hskip 2.0pt\mathsl{mn}[K]\big(c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$=\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$={\kern-6.45831pt}\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle={\kern-0.60275pt}\ll{\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle=\ll$}}}\omega\big)(\psi)\\[1.99997pt]
\mathsl{D}_{\mathsl{KL}}\big(\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi),\,c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$=\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$={\kern-6.45831pt}\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle={\kern-0.60275pt}\ll{\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle=\ll$}}}\omega^{\prime}\big)&\,\leq&\mathsl{D}_{\mathsl{KL}}\big(\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi),\,c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$=\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$={\kern-6.45831pt}\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle={\kern-0.60275pt}\ll{\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle=\ll$}}}\omega\big).\end{array}

4.

The Pearl
update of
\omega
with
\psi
along the channel
c
satisfies:

\begin{array}[]{rcccl}\omega{\kern 1.29167pt}\raisebox{-6.02773pt}{$\scalebox{1.0}{\hbox to2.28pt{\vbox to11.07pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip-14.02638pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}}{}

{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 19.69 13.19 C 20.17 13.77 21.04 13.85 21.63 13.36 C 22.21 12.87 22.29 12 21.8 11.42 C 21.48 11.04 20.99 10.86 20.5 10.95 C 20.18 11 19.89 11.17 19.69 11.42}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}$}{\kern-0.86108pt}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{$\scriptstyle c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\ggg{\kern-4.52083pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\ggg{\kern-4.52083pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 1.50694pt}\scriptstyle\ggg{\kern-0.42192pt}={\kern 1.05486pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\ggg=$}}}\psi$}&=&\omega\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$|$}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsl{mn}[K](c)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg{\kern-4.52083pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 1.50694pt}\scriptstyle\gg{\kern-0.42192pt}={\kern 1.05486pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\gg=$}}}\mathbf{1}_{\psi}$}&=&\mathsl{mn}[K](c)^{{\dagger}}_{\omega}(\psi).\end{array}

Writing
\omega^{\prime}=\omega\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$|$}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsl{mn}[K](c)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg{\kern-4.52083pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 1.50694pt}\scriptstyle\gg{\kern-0.42192pt}={\kern 1.05486pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\gg=$}}}\mathbf{1}_{\psi}$}
we get:

\begin{array}[]{rcccccl}\big(\mathsl{mn}[K](c)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$=\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$={\kern-6.45831pt}\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle={\kern-0.60275pt}\ll{\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle=\ll$}}}\omega^{\prime}\big)(\psi)&\,=&\omega^{\prime}\mathbin{\smash{\models_{\hskip-3.61664pt{\raisebox{-1.20552pt}{\tiny J}}}}\,}c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\ggg{\kern-6.45831pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\ggg{\kern-6.45831pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle\ggg{\kern-0.60275pt}={\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\ggg=$}}}\psi&\,\geq&\omega\mathbin{\smash{\models_{\hskip-3.61664pt{\raisebox{-1.20552pt}{\tiny J}}}}\,}c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\ggg{\kern-6.45831pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\ggg{\kern-6.45831pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle\ggg{\kern-0.60275pt}={\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\ggg=$}}}\psi&\,=&\big(\mathsl{mn}[K](c)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$=\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$={\kern-6.45831pt}\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle={\kern-0.60275pt}\ll{\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle=\ll$}}}\omega\big)(\psi).\end{array}

Proof

1.

First, the Jeffrey validity can be rewritten as:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\omega\mathbin{\smash{\models_{\hskip-3.61664pt{\raisebox{-1.20552pt}{\tiny J}}}}\,}c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\ggg{\kern-6.45831pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\ggg{\kern-6.45831pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle\ggg{\kern-0.60275pt}={\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\ggg=$}}}\psi&\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{JeffreyValidityAlong}}}{{=}}}&\displaystyle({\kern 1.0pt}\psi{\kern 1.0pt})\cdot\prod_{y\in Y}\,\big(c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$=\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$={\kern-6.45831pt}\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle={\kern-0.60275pt}\ll{\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle=\ll$}}}\omega\models\mathbf{1}_{y}\big)^{\psi(y)}\\[10.00002pt]
&=&\displaystyle({\kern 1.0pt}\psi{\kern 1.0pt})\cdot\prod_{y\in Y}\,\big((c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$=\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$={\kern-6.45831pt}\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle={\kern-0.60275pt}\ll{\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle=\ll$}}}\omega)(y)\big)^{\psi(y)}\hskip 2.0pt\,\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{MultinomialEqn}}}{{=}}}\,\hskip 2.0pt\mathsl{mn}[K]\big(c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$=\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$={\kern-6.45831pt}\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle={\kern-0.60275pt}\ll{\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle=\ll$}}}\omega\big)(\psi).\end{array}

2.

For the Pearl validity we get:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\omega\mathbin{\smash{\models_{\hskip-3.61664pt{\raisebox{-1.20552pt}{\tiny P}}}}\,}c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\ggg{\kern-6.45831pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\ggg{\kern-6.45831pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle\ggg{\kern-0.60275pt}={\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\ggg=$}}}\psi&\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{PearlValidityAlong}}}{{=}}}&\displaystyle({\kern 1.0pt}\psi{\kern 1.0pt})\cdot\Big(\omega\models\mathop{\textnormal{\Large\&}}_{y\in Y}\,(c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$=\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$={\kern-6.45831pt}\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle={\kern-0.60275pt}\ll{\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle=\ll$}}}\mathbf{1}_{y})^{\psi(y)}\Big)\\[10.00002pt]
&=&\displaystyle({\kern 1.0pt}\psi{\kern 1.0pt})\cdot\sum_{x\in X}\,\omega(x)\cdot\prod_{y\in Y}\,(c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg{\kern-6.45831pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle\gg{\kern-0.60275pt}={\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\gg=$}}}\mathbf{1}_{y})(x)^{\psi(y)}\\[10.00002pt]
&=&\displaystyle\sum_{x\in X}\,\omega(x)\cdot({\kern 1.0pt}\psi{\kern 1.0pt})\cdot\prod_{y\in Y}\,c(x)(y)^{\psi(y)}\\[10.00002pt]
&\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{MultinomialEqn}}}{{=}}}&\displaystyle\sum_{x\in X}\,\omega(x)\cdot\mathsl{mn}[K]\big(c(x)\big)(\psi)\\[3.99994pt]
&=&\big(\mathsl{mn}[K](c)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$=\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$={\kern-6.45831pt}\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle={\kern-0.60275pt}\ll{\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle=\ll$}}}\omega\big)(\psi),\qquad\mbox{where }\mathsl{mn}[K](c)=\mathsl{mn}[K]\mathrel{\circ}c.\end{array}

3.

We move to the Jeffrey update and compute:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\omega{\kern-0.86108pt}\raisebox{-5.16663pt}{$\scalebox{0.9}{\hbox to2.68pt{\vbox to12.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip-11.75017pt\lower-1.3381pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 19.69 0 L 19.69 14.76}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}{{{{}{}{}{}}}{{}{}{}{}}}{}
{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 19.69 0 C 19.69 -0.87 18.98 -1.57 18.11 -1.57 C 17.24 -1.57 16.54 -0.87 16.54 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}$}{\kern 0.86108pt}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{$\scriptstyle c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\ggg{\kern-4.52083pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\ggg{\kern-4.52083pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 1.50694pt}\scriptstyle\ggg{\kern-0.42192pt}={\kern 1.05486pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\ggg=$}}}\psi$}&\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{EvicenceUpdateDefJeffreyEqn}}}{{=}}}&\displaystyle\sum_{y\in Y}\,\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)(y)\cdot\omega\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$|$}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{$\scriptstyle c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg{\kern-4.52083pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 1.50694pt}\scriptstyle\gg{\kern-0.42192pt}={\kern 1.05486pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\gg=$}}}\mathbf{1}_{y}$}\\[10.00002pt]
&=&\displaystyle\sum_{y\in Y}\,\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)(y)\cdot c^{{\dagger}}_{\omega}(y)\hskip 2.0pt=\hskip 2.0ptc^{{\dagger}}_{\omega}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$=\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$={\kern-6.45831pt}\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle={\kern-0.60275pt}\ll{\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle=\ll$}}}\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi).\end{array}

The validity increases and divergence decreases mentioned
above follow directly from
Theorem
5.2
(
1
),
(
2
).

4.

For Pearl update with point evidence we have:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\omega\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$|$}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathsl{mn}[K](c)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg{\kern-4.52083pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 1.50694pt}\scriptstyle\gg{\kern-0.42192pt}={\kern 1.05486pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\gg=$}}}\mathbf{1}_{\psi}$}&\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{UpdateEqn}}}{{=}}}&\displaystyle\sum_{x\in X}\,\frac{\omega(x)\cdot(\mathsl{mn}[K](c)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg{\kern-6.45831pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle\gg{\kern-0.60275pt}={\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\gg=$}}}\mathbf{1}_{\psi})(x)}{\omega\models\mathsl{mn}[K](c)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg{\kern-6.45831pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle\gg{\kern-0.60275pt}={\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\gg=$}}}\mathbf{1}_{\psi}}\,\big|{\kern 1.00006pt}x{\kern 1.00006pt}\big\rangle\\[10.00002pt]
&=&\displaystyle\sum_{x\in X}\,\frac{\omega(x)\cdot\mathsl{mn}[K](c(x))(\psi)}{(\mathsl{mn}[K](c)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$=\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$={\kern-6.45831pt}\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle={\kern-0.60275pt}\ll{\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle=\ll$}}}\omega)(\psi)}\,\big|{\kern 1.00006pt}x{\kern 1.00006pt}\big\rangle\\[10.00002pt]
&=&\displaystyle\sum_{x\in X}\,\frac{\omega(x)\cdot({\kern 1.0pt}\psi{\kern 1.0pt})\cdot\prod_{y}c(x)(y)^{\psi(y)}}{\sum_{x}\omega(x)\cdot({\kern 1.0pt}\psi{\kern 1.0pt})\cdot\prod_{y}c(x)(y)^{\psi(y)}}\,\big|{\kern 1.00006pt}x{\kern 1.00006pt}\big\rangle\\[10.00002pt]
&=&\displaystyle\sum_{x\in X}\,\frac{\omega(x)\cdot(\mathrel{\&}_{y}(c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg{\kern-6.45831pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle\gg{\kern-0.60275pt}={\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\gg=$}}}\mathbf{1}_{y})^{\psi(y)})(x)}{\sum_{x}\omega(x)\cdot(\mathrel{\&}_{y}(c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg{\kern-6.45831pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle\gg{\kern-0.60275pt}={\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\gg=$}}}\mathbf{1}_{y})^{\psi(y)})(x)}\,\big|{\kern 1.00006pt}x{\kern 1.00006pt}\big\rangle\\[10.00002pt]
&\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{UpdateEqn}}}{{=}}}&\omega\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$|$}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathrel{\&}_{y}(c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg{\kern-4.52083pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 1.50694pt}\scriptstyle\gg{\kern-0.42192pt}={\kern 1.05486pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\gg=$}}}\mathbf{1}_{y})^{\psi(y)}$}\\[1.99997pt]
&=&\omega{\kern 1.29167pt}\raisebox{-6.02773pt}{$\scalebox{1.0}{\hbox to2.28pt{\vbox to11.07pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip-14.02638pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 19.69 0 L 19.69 14.76}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}{{{{}{}{}{}}}{{{}{}{}{}}}{{{}{}{}{}}}{{}{}{}{}}}{}
{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 19.69 13.19 C 20.17 13.77 21.04 13.85 21.63 13.36 C 22.21 12.87 22.29 12 21.8 11.42 C 21.48 11.04 20.99 10.86 20.5 10.95 C 20.18 11 19.89 11.17 19.69 11.42}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}$}{\kern-0.86108pt}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{$\scriptstyle c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\ggg{\kern-4.52083pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\ggg{\kern-4.52083pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 1.50694pt}\scriptstyle\ggg{\kern-0.42192pt}={\kern 1.05486pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\ggg=$}}}\psi$}.\end{array}

The claimed increase of multinomial probability follows from
Theorem
5.3
(
1
).

7
A glance at updating in predictive coding

The naive picture of human learning involves a teacher pouring
knowledge into a student’s brain — as visually expressed by what is
called a Nuremberg Funnel. One more modern approach in (computational)
cognitive science is called predictive coding (or processing), see

e.g.
the books
[
6
,
16
,
29
]
or
articles
[
1
,
12
]
. Very briefly, the idea is that
the human mind projects, evaluates and updates. Predictive coding
describes the mind basically as a prediction engine that compares its
predictions to observations, leading to internal adaptations. To quote
Friston
[
11
]
: “The Bayesian brain hypothesis uses
Bayesian probability theory to formulate perception as a constructive
process based on internal or generative models. […] In this view,
the brain is an inference machine that actively predicts and explains
its sensations. Central to this hypothesis is a probabilistic model
that can generate predictions, against which sensory samples are
tested to update beliefs about their causes.” This testing involves
error reduction: “…the core function of the brain is simply to
minimize prediction error, where the prediction errors signal
mismatches between predicted input and the input actually
received.”
[
27
]
. Mathematically, these prediction
errors can be expressed in terms of KL-divergence (
19
),
see
e.g.

[
27
,
32
]
. The aimed decrease of
prediction errors / KL-divergence is a form of what we call getting
wiser. Here we concentrate on this error correction aspect of
predictive coding. There is however, a wider story, especially about
active inference
[
29
]
.

When the human mind is understood as a Bayesian inference engine, the
question comes up: how does the mind handle multiple observations, via
the rules of Jeffrey or Pearl? Predictive coding emphasises error
correction, which, in terms of reducing KL-divergence, is achieved via
the update rule of Jeffrey (for point evidence), see
Theorem
5.2
(
2
)
and
Proposition
1
(
3
). This
suggests that the mind is a Jeffreyan update engine. A further
indication is that Jeffrey’s updating is sensitive to the order of
updating, see
Theorem
5.2
(
4
).
As is well-known, humans are very sensitive to the order in which they
process information (are ‘primed’). On the other hand, recall that in
the medical example in the beginning we associated Pearl’s approach
with a clinical perspective, where tests are applied to the same
person. This fits a Pearlian perspective in predictive coding where
updates apply (dependently) to the same mind, of a single individual.
In predictive coding the updates of Jeffrey and Pearl are not
(explicitly) used, but an approximation of Jeffrey, called VFE update,
where VFE is an abbreviation of variational free energy. Below we
briefly put this VFE rule in the setting of predictive coding, based
on
[
19
,
37
]
.

Updating in predictive coding happens via variational inference, in
order to minimalise free energy
[
11
]
. This is an
approximation technique, which in this case produces VFE updating as
an approximation of Jeffrey updating. As will be shown, VFE does not
inherit the crucial KL-divergence reduction from Jeffrey, but it does
increase Pearl validity — although less than Pearl updating
does. One can conclude that both Jeffrey and Pearl updating outperform
VFE updating.

Later on, in Remark
3
we go into the details of what free
energy amounts to in the setting of predictive coding. At this stage
we first provide the description of the VFE update, in
item (
) below. It uses a softmax description,
involving normalisation of
e
-powers. This is used more widely in
cognitive modeling, see
e.g.

[
36
]
. The
description that we use is based on
[
37
, Eqn. (44)]
. The
subsequent minimality characterisation in
item (
2
) occurs (in essence)
in
[
32
,
37
]
.

Definition 9

Let
\omega\in\mathcal{D}(X)
be a distribution with evidence

\psi\in\mathcal{M}\big(\mathsl{Fact}(X)\big)
. The VFE update

\omega{\kern 1.29167pt}\raisebox{-6.02773pt}{$\scalebox{1.0}{\hbox to3.25pt{\vbox to11.07pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip-14.02638pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 19.69 0 L 19.69 14.76}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 19.69 13.78 L 23.62 13.78}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 19.69 11.81 L 22.64 11.81}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}$}{\kern-1.07639pt}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{$\scriptstyle\psi$}
is defined as normalised ‘softmax’ of the form:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\omega{\kern 1.29167pt}\raisebox{-6.02773pt}{$\scalebox{1.0}{\hbox to3.25pt{\vbox to11.07pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip-14.02638pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 19.69 0 L 19.69 14.76}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 19.69 13.78 L 23.62 13.78}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 19.69 11.81 L 22.64 11.81}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}$}{\kern-1.07639pt}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{$\scriptstyle\psi$}&\coloneqq&\displaystyle\mathsl{Flrn}\left(\sum_{x\in\mathsl{supp}(\omega)}\,e^{\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)\models\ln(\omega\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$|$}\raisebox{-2.1097pt}{$\scriptstyle(-)$}(x))}\big|{\kern 1.00006pt}x{\kern 1.00006pt}\big\rangle\right).\end{array}

(30)

The letter ‘F’ in the sign

refers to
‘free’ in free energy.

We immediately give an alternative description of VFE updating,
together with its characterisation in terms of minimal free energy.
In item (
1
) below we stretch earlier notation
since we use a predicate power
p^{f}
for a fraction
f
and not for
a natural number
n
, as orignally in
Definition
3
(
1
).
But the meaning is the same, given pointwise as
p^{f}(x)=p(x)^{f}
.
Similarly, we use the conjunction
\mathop{\mathrel{\&}}
not only for multisets

\mathop{\mathrel{\&}}(\psi)
but also for distributions

\mathop{\mathrel{\&}}\big(\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)\big)
, where:

\begin{array}[]{rcccl}\mathop{\mathrel{\&}}\big(\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)\big)(x)&=&\displaystyle\prod_{p\in\mathsl{supp}(\psi)}p^{\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)(p)}(x)&=&\displaystyle\prod_{p\in\mathsl{supp}(\psi)}p(x)^{\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)(p)}.\end{array}

2.

as a minimum below. This is the minimum free energy, see

below.

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\omega{\kern 1.29167pt}\raisebox{-6.02773pt}{$\scalebox{1.0}{\hbox to3.25pt{\vbox to11.07pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip-14.02638pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}}{}

&&\hskip 2.0pt=\hskip 2.0pt\displaystyle\argmin\limits_{\rho\in\mathcal{D}(X)}\,\sum_{p\in\mathsl{supp}(\psi)}\,\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)(p)\cdot\mathsl{D}_{\mathsl{KL}}\big(\rho,\,\omega\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$|$}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{$\scriptstyle p$}\big).\end{array}

Here we consider
\argmin
as giving a subset of outcomes,
since there may be multiple distributions with the same minimum value.

Notice that the VFE update (

) looks very much

\omega{\kern 1.29167pt}\raisebox{-6.02773pt}{$\scalebox{1.0}{\hbox to2.28pt{\vbox to11.07pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip-14.02638pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}}{}

in (

\omega{\kern 1.29167pt}\raisebox{-6.02773pt}{$\scalebox{1.0}{\hbox to2.28pt{\vbox to11.07pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip-14.02638pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}}{}

. The VFE and Pearl
update behave similarly, see Theorem
7.1
below.

Proof

1.

Using the familiar equations
e^{a+b}=e^{a}\cdot e^{b}
and
e^{a\cdot b}=\big(e^{a}\big)^{b}
we observe that we can write the
e
-expression
in (
30
) as:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\lx@intercol\lx@add@cssclass{ltx_eqn_lefteqn}$\displaystyle e^{\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)\models\ln(\omega\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$|$}\raisebox{-2.1097pt}{$\scriptstyle(-)$}(x))}$\mbox{}\hfil\lx@intercol\\[1.99997pt]
&=&e^{\sum_{p\in\mathsl{supp}(\psi)}\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)(p)\cdot\ln(\omega\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$|$}\raisebox{-2.1097pt}{$\scriptstyle p$}(x))}\\
&=&\displaystyle\prod_{p\in\mathsl{supp}(\psi)}\,e^{\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)(p)\cdot\ln(\omega\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$|$}\raisebox{-2.1097pt}{$\scriptstyle p$}(x))}\\
&=&\displaystyle\prod_{p\in\mathsl{supp}(\psi)}\,\Big(e^{\ln(\omega\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$|$}\raisebox{-2.1097pt}{$\scriptstyle p$}(x))}\Big)^{\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)(p)}\\
&=&\displaystyle\prod_{p\in\mathsl{supp}(\psi)}\,\Big(\omega\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$|$}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{$\scriptstyle p$}(x)\Big)^{\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)(p)}\\
&\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{UpdateEqn}}}{{=}}}&\displaystyle\prod_{p\in\mathsl{supp}(\psi)}\,\left(\frac{\omega(x)\cdot p(x)}{\omega\models p}\right)^{\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)(p)}\\[13.99995pt]
&=&\displaystyle\omega(x)^{\sum_{p\in\mathsl{supp}(\psi)}\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)(p)}\cdot\prod_{p\in\mathsl{supp}(\psi)}\,p(x)^{\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)(p)}\cdot\frac{1}{\prod_{p\in\mathsl{supp}(\psi)}(\omega\models p)^{\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)(p)}}\\[13.99995pt]
&\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{JeffreyValidity}}}{{=}}}&\displaystyle\omega(x)\cdot\big(\mathop{\mathrel{\&}}\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)\big)(x)\cdot\left(\frac{({\kern 1.0pt}\psi{\kern 1.0pt})}{\omega\mathbin{\smash{\models_{\hskip-3.61664pt{\raisebox{-1.20552pt}{\tiny J}}}}\,}\psi}\right)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{\|\psi\|}}}.\end{array}

Using this formulation we obtain Equation (
30
):

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\lx@intercol\lx@add@cssclass{ltx_eqn_lefteqn}$\displaystyle\mathsl{Flrn}\left(\sum_{x\in\mathsl{supp}(\omega)}\,e^{\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)\models\ln(\omega\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$|$}\raisebox{-2.1097pt}{$\scriptstyle(-)$}(x))}\big|{\kern 1.00006pt}x{\kern 1.00006pt}\big\rangle\right)(z)$\mbox{}\hfil\lx@intercol\\[13.99995pt]
&=&\displaystyle\frac{e^{\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)\models\ln(\omega\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$|$}\raisebox{-2.1097pt}{$\scriptstyle(-)$}(z))}}{\sum_{y}\,e^{\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)\models\ln(\omega\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$|$}\raisebox{-2.1097pt}{$\scriptstyle(-)$}(y))}}\\[13.99995pt]
&=&\displaystyle\frac{\omega(z)\cdot(\mathop{\mathrel{\&}}\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi))(z)}{\sum_{y}\,\omega(y)\cdot(\mathop{\mathrel{\&}}\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi))(y)}\qquad\mbox{ as just shown}\\[11.99998pt]
&\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{UpdateEqn}}}{{=}}}&\omega\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$|$}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathop{\mathrel{\&}}\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)$}(z)\\[3.99994pt]
&\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{EnergyUpdateAltEqn}}}{{=}}}&\omega{\kern 1.29167pt}\raisebox{-6.02773pt}{$\scalebox{1.0}{\hbox to3.25pt{\vbox to11.07pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip-14.02638pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 19.69 0 L 19.69 14.76}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 19.69 13.78 L 23.62 13.78}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 19.69 11.81 L 22.64 11.81}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}$}{\kern-1.07639pt}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{$\scriptstyle\psi$}(z).\end{array}

2.

Let the normalisation factor in (
30
) be
s
,
so that
s=\sum_{x}e^{\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)\models\ln(\omega\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$|$}\raisebox{-2.1097pt}{$\scriptstyle(-)$}(x))}
. For an arbitrary distribution

\rho\in\mathcal{D}(X)
we compute the value of the expression
in (
32
).

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\lx@intercol\lx@add@cssclass{ltx_eqn_lefteqn}$\displaystyle\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)\models\mathsl{D}_{\mathsl{KL}}\big(\rho,\omega\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$|$}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{$\scriptstyle(-)$}\big)$\mbox{}\hfil\lx@intercol\\
&=&\displaystyle\sum_{p\in\mathsl{supp}(\psi)}\,\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)(p)\cdot\sum_{x\in X}\,\rho(x)\cdot\ln\left(\frac{\rho(x)}{\omega\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$|$}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{$\scriptstyle p$}(x)}\right)\\
&=&\displaystyle\sum_{x\in X}\rho(x)\cdot\left[\ln\big(\rho(x)\big)-\sum_{p\in\mathsl{supp}(\psi)}\,\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)(p)\cdot\ln\big(\omega\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$|$}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{$\scriptstyle p$}(x)\big)\right]\\[13.99995pt]
&=&\displaystyle\sum_{x\in X}\rho(x)\cdot\left[\ln\big(\rho(x)\big)-\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)\models\ln\big(\omega\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$|$}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{$\scriptstyle(-)$}(x)\big)\right]\\[10.00002pt]
&=&\displaystyle\sum_{x\in X}\rho(x)\cdot\left[\ln\big(\rho(x)\big)-\ln\big(e^{\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)\models\ln\big(\omega\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$|$}\raisebox{-2.1097pt}{$\scriptstyle(-)$}(x)\big)}\big)\right]\\[10.00002pt]
&\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{EnergyUpdateEqn}}}{{=}}}&\displaystyle\sum_{x\in X}\rho(x)\cdot\Big[\ln\big(\rho(x)\big)-\ln\big(s\cdot\omega{\kern 1.29167pt}\raisebox{-6.02773pt}{$\scalebox{1.0}{\hbox to3.25pt{\vbox to11.07pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip-14.02638pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 19.69 0 L 19.69 14.76}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 19.69 13.78 L 23.62 13.78}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 19.69 11.81 L 22.64 11.81}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}$}{\kern-1.07639pt}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{$\scriptstyle\psi$}(x)\big)\Big]\\[1.99997pt]
&=&\mathsl{D}_{\mathsl{KL}}\big(\rho,\,\omega{\kern 1.29167pt}\raisebox{-6.02773pt}{$\scalebox{1.0}{\hbox to3.25pt{\vbox to11.07pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip-14.02638pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 19.69 0 L 19.69 14.76}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 19.69 13.78 L 23.62 13.78}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 19.69 11.81 L 22.64 11.81}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}$}{\kern-1.07639pt}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{$\scriptstyle\psi$}\big)-\ln(s).\end{array}

Remark 3

It may not be immediately clear that the VFE update defined
in (
30
) corresponds to what is used in predictive
coding. Therefore we elaborate this relationship more explicitly,
using the setting of
[
37
]
. There, two sets
S
and
O
are
used for hidden elements and for observations in a generative
model. It is given by a joint distribution
M\in\mathcal{D}(S\times O)
,
together with a distribution
o\in\mathcal{D}(O)
of observations. The latter
corresponds to point evidence in our situation, so that we can think
of it as
o=\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)
for point evidence
\psi\in\mathcal{M}(O)
.

The joint distribution
M\in\mathcal{D}(S\times O)
may be
disintegrated
[
4
]
into a form
M=\langle\mathsl{id},c\rangle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$=\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$={\kern-6.45831pt}\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle={\kern-0.60275pt}\ll{\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle=\ll$}}}\omega
, for a channel
c\colon S\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\to$\cr\hfil\!$\hbox{\hbox to2.2pt{\vbox to2.2pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.09999pt\lower-1.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{\ignorespaces}}
{{{}}{\ignorespaces}{}{\ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#FFFFFF} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#FFFFFF} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.97 0 C 0.97 0.53 0.53 0.97 0 0.97 C -0.53 0.97 -0.97 0.53 -0.97 0 C -0.97 -0.53 -0.53 -0.97 0 -0.97 C 0.53 -0.97 0.97 -0.53 0.97 0 Z M 0 0}{} \ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}{\ignorespaces}{}{\ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#FFFFFF} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.2pt} \ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 1.38 0 C 1.38 0.76 0.76 1.38 0 1.38 C -0.76 1.38 -1.38 0.76 -1.38 0 C -1.38 -0.76 -0.76 -1.38 0 -1.38 C 0.76 -1.38 1.38 -0.76 1.38 0 Z M 0 0}{} \ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\ignorespaces}{\ignorespaces}{\ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}$\hfil\cr}}}O
and a distribution

\omega\in\mathcal{D}(S)
. We write
d
for the reversal / dagger of the
channel
c
, of the form
d=c_{\omega}^{{\dagger}}\colon O\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\to$\cr\hfil\!$\hbox{\hbox to2.2pt{\vbox to2.2pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.09999pt\lower-1.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{\ignorespaces}}
{{{}}{\ignorespaces}{}{\ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#FFFFFF} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#FFFFFF} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.97 0 C 0.97 0.53 0.53 0.97 0 0.97 C -0.53 0.97 -0.97 0.53 -0.97 0 C -0.97 -0.53 -0.53 -0.97 0 -0.97 C 0.53 -0.97 0.97 -0.53 0.97 0 Z M 0 0}{} \ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}{\ignorespaces}{}{\ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#FFFFFF} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.2pt} \ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 1.38 0 C 1.38 0.76 0.76 1.38 0 1.38 C -0.76 1.38 -1.38 0.76 -1.38 0 C -1.38 -0.76 -0.76 -1.38 0 -1.38 C 0.76 -1.38 1.38 -0.76 1.38 0 Z M 0 0}{} \ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\ignorespaces}{\ignorespaces}{\ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}$\hfil\cr}}}S
,
so that
d(y)(s)=\big(\omega\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$|$}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{$\scriptstyle c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg{\kern-4.52083pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 1.50694pt}\scriptstyle\gg{\kern-0.42192pt}={\kern 1.05486pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\gg=$}}}\mathbf{1}_{y}$}\big)(s)
, see
Proposition
1
(
3
). In
[
37
]

this dagger
d
is written as
M(s|y)
.

The VFE-update rule considered in
[
37
]
, in Equations (44)
and (45), produces a posterior, via normalisation, written as:

\displaystyle\NormOp\limits_{s}\,\left(e^{\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{y\sim o}\ln(M(s|y))}\right).

(33)

In our setting we write frequentist learning
\mathsl{Flrn}
for the
normalisation and validity
\models
for the expectation

\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{x\sim o}
. This turns (
33
) into:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\displaystyle\mathsl{Flrn}\left(\sum_{s\in S}\,e^{o\models\ln(d(-)(s))}\,\big|{\kern 1.00006pt}s{\kern 1.00006pt}\big\rangle\right)&=&\displaystyle\mathsl{Flrn}\left(\sum_{s\in S}\,e^{o\models\ln\big((\omega\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$|$}\raisebox{-2.1097pt}{$\scriptstyle c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg{\kern-3.16457pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 1.05486pt}\scriptstyle\gg{\kern-0.29535pt}={\kern 0.73839pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\gg=$}}}\mathbf{1}_{(-)}$})(s)\big)}\,\big|{\kern 1.00006pt}s{\kern 1.00006pt}\big\rangle\right).\end{array}

The VFE update (
30
) is a generalisation
of the latter formula, in the following way. The evidence that is
used for updating
\omega
is not of the pullback form
c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg{\kern-6.45831pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle\gg{\kern-0.60275pt}={\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\gg=$}}}\mathbf{1}_{(-)}
, but is a general factor
p
. This factor comes from the
evidence
o
, which is generalised from point evidence, to arbitrary
evidence
o=\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)
, for
\psi\in\mathcal{M}\big(\mathsl{Fact}(O)\big)
.

Having explained the formula (
30
), we turn to the
associated free energy formulation. It can also be translated to the
current setting, again starting from
[
37
]
. There, the free
energy of a distribution
\rho\in\mathcal{D}(S)
, is defined with respect to
the generative model
M\in\mathcal{D}(S\times O)
and the distribution of
observations
o\in\mathcal{D}(O)
. It involves the dagger
M(s|y)
that we
mentioned before and takes the explicit form:

\begin{array}[]{rcccccccccl}\big(\omega|_{c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg{\kern-4.52083pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 1.50694pt}\scriptstyle\gg{\kern-0.42192pt}={\kern 1.05486pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\gg=$}}}\mathbf{1}_{y}}\big)(s)&=&c_{\omega}^{{\dagger}}(y)(s)&=&d(y)(s)&=&M(s|y)&=&\displaystyle\frac{M(s,y)}{M(y)}&=&\displaystyle\frac{M(s,y)}{(c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$=\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$={\kern-6.45831pt}\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle={\kern-0.60275pt}\ll{\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle=\ll$}}}\omega)(y)}.\end{array}

What is then called free energy of
\rho
is computed as:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\lx@intercol\lx@add@cssclass{ltx_eqn_lefteqn}$\displaystyle\sum_{s\in S,\,y\in O}\,\rho(s)\cdot o(y)\cdot\ln\left(\frac{\rho(s)}{M(s,y)}\right)$\mbox{}\hfil\lx@intercol\\[1.99997pt]
&=&\displaystyle\sum_{s\in S,\,y\in O}\,\rho(s)\cdot o(y)\cdot\ln\left(\frac{\rho(s)\cdot(c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$=\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$={\kern-6.45831pt}\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle={\kern-0.60275pt}\ll{\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle=\ll$}}}\omega)(y)}{(\omega|_{c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg{\kern-4.52083pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 1.50694pt}\scriptstyle\gg{\kern-0.42192pt}={\kern 1.05486pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\gg=$}}}\mathbf{1}_{y}})(s)}\right)\\
&=&\displaystyle\sum_{y\in O}o(y)\cdot\sum_{s\in S}\,\rho(s)\cdot\ln\left(\frac{\rho(s)}{(\omega|_{c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg{\kern-4.52083pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 1.50694pt}\scriptstyle\gg{\kern-0.42192pt}={\kern 1.05486pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\gg=$}}}\mathbf{1}_{y}})(s)}\right)\,-\,\sum_{y\in O}\,o(y)\cdot\ln\big((c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$=\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$={\kern-6.45831pt}\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle={\kern-0.60275pt}\ll{\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle=\ll$}}}\omega)(y)\big)\\[11.99998pt]
&=&\displaystyle o\models\mathsl{D}_{\mathsl{KL}}\big(\rho,\,\omega|_{c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg{\kern-4.52083pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 1.50694pt}\scriptstyle\gg{\kern-0.42192pt}={\kern 1.05486pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\gg=$}}}\mathbf{1}_{(-)}}\big)\,-\,o\models\ln\big((c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$=\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$={\kern-6.45831pt}\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle={\kern-0.60275pt}\ll{\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle=\ll$}}}\omega)(-)\big).\end{array}

The latter term does not depend on
\rho
and can be ignored
when we wish to minimise. The VFE-update from
[
37
]
is the
argmin of the first divergence-validity expression. Translated to the
current setting it occurs in
Proposition
2
(
2
), again
with the distribution
o
replaced by
\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)
and the factor
c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\gg{\kern-6.45831pt}=$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle\gg{\kern-0.60275pt}={\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\gg=$}}}\mathbf{1}_{(-)}
by
p\in\mathsl{supp}(\psi)
.

We like to put the expression after
\argmin

in (
32
) in a wider perspective. The first
inequality below stems from
[
37
, Eqn (41)]
.

Lemma 8

1.

For a channel
c\colon Z\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\to$\cr\hfil\!$\hbox{\hbox to2.2pt{\vbox to2.2pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.09999pt\lower-1.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{\ignorespaces}}
{{{}}{\ignorespaces}{}{\ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#FFFFFF} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#FFFFFF} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.97 0 C 0.97 0.53 0.53 0.97 0 0.97 C -0.53 0.97 -0.97 0.53 -0.97 0 C -0.97 -0.53 -0.53 -0.97 0 -0.97 C 0.53 -0.97 0.97 -0.53 0.97 0 Z M 0 0}{} \ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}{\ignorespaces}{}{\ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#FFFFFF} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.2pt} \ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 1.38 0 C 1.38 0.76 0.76 1.38 0 1.38 C -0.76 1.38 -1.38 0.76 -1.38 0 C -1.38 -0.76 -0.76 -1.38 0 -1.38 C 0.76 -1.38 1.38 -0.76 1.38 0 Z M 0 0}{} \ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\ignorespaces}{\ignorespaces}{\ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}$\hfil\cr}}}X
and
two distributions
\sigma\in\mathcal{D}(Z)
and
\rho\in\mathcal{D}(X)
there is
an inequality of the form:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\sigma\models\mathsl{D}_{\mathsl{KL}}\big(\rho,\,c(-)\big)&\,\geq&\mathsl{D}_{\mathsl{KL}}\big(\rho,\,c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$=\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$={\kern-6.45831pt}\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle={\kern-0.60275pt}\ll{\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle=\ll$}}}\sigma\big).\end{array}

2.

When we apply this inequality to
the expression in (
32
) for distributions

with evidence

\psi\in\mathcal{M}\big(\mathsl{Fact}(X)\big)
, we see that the KL-divergence
between

and the Jeffrey update
\omega{\kern-0.86108pt}\raisebox{-5.16663pt}{$\scalebox{0.9}{\hbox to2.68pt{\vbox to12.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip-11.75017pt\lower-1.3381pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}$}{\kern 0.86108pt}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{$\scriptstyle\psi$}
serves
as lower bound for the minimisation

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)\models\mathsl{D}_{\mathsl{KL}}\big(\rho,\,\omega\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$|$}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{$\scriptstyle(-)$}\big)&\,\geq&\mathsl{D}_{\mathsl{KL}}\big(\rho,\,\omega{\kern-0.86108pt}\raisebox{-5.16663pt}{$\scalebox{0.9}{\hbox to2.68pt{\vbox to12.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip-11.75017pt\lower-1.3381pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}}{}

{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 19.69 0 C 19.69 -0.87 18.98 -1.57 18.11 -1.57 C 17.24 -1.57 16.54 -0.87 16.54 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}$}{\kern 0.86108pt}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{$\scriptstyle\psi$}\big).\end{array}

(34)

Proof

1.

We consider the minus on both sides and use Jensen’s inequality in:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}-\Big(\sigma\models\mathsl{D}_{\mathsl{KL}}\big(\rho,c(-)\big)\Big)&=&-\displaystyle\sum_{z\in Z}\sigma(z)\cdot\sum_{x\in X}\rho(x)\cdot\ln\left(\frac{\rho(x)}{c(z)(x)}\right)\\
&=&\displaystyle\sum_{x\in X}\rho(x)\cdot\sum_{z\in Z}\sigma(z)\cdot\ln\left(\frac{c(z)(x)}{\rho(x)}\right)\\
&\leq&\displaystyle\sum_{x\in X}\rho(x)\cdot\ln\left(\sum_{z\in Z}\sigma(z)\cdot\frac{c(z)(x)}{\rho(x)}\right)\\[13.99995pt]
&=&\displaystyle\sum_{x\in X}\rho(x)\cdot\ln\left(\frac{(c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$=\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$={\kern-6.45831pt}\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle={\kern-0.60275pt}\ll{\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle=\ll$}}}\sigma)(x)}{\rho(x)}\right)\\
&=&-\mathsl{D}_{\mathsl{KL}}\big(\rho,c\mathrel{\mathchoice{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$=\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$={\kern-6.45831pt}\ll$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{${\kern 2.15277pt}\scriptstyle={\kern-0.60275pt}\ll{\kern 1.50694pt}$}}{\scalebox{-0.5}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle=\ll$}}}\sigma\big).\end{array}

2.

By using the mapping
p\mapsto\omega\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$|$}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{$\scriptstyle p$}
as function /
channel
\mathsl{Fact}(X)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(X)
, with distribution

\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)\in\mathcal{D}\big(\mathsl{Fact}(X)\big)
we get:

\begin{array}[b]{rcl}\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)\models\mathsl{D}_{\mathsl{KL}}\big(\rho,\,\omega\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$|$}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{$\scriptstyle(-)$}\big)&\,\geq&\displaystyle\mathsl{D}_{\mathsl{KL}}\left(\rho,\,\sum_{p\in\mathsl{supp}(\psi)}\mathsl{Flrn}(\psi)(p)\cdot\omega\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$|$}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{$\scriptstyle p$}\right)\\[13.99995pt]
&\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{EvicenceUpdateDefJeffreyEqn}}}{{=}}}&\mathsl{D}_{\mathsl{KL}}\big(\rho,\,\omega{\kern-0.86108pt}\raisebox{-5.16663pt}{$\scalebox{0.9}{\hbox to2.68pt{\vbox to12.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip-11.75017pt\lower-1.3381pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 19.69 0 L 19.69 14.76}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}{{{{}{}{}{}}}{{}{}{}{}}}{}
{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 19.69 0 C 19.69 -0.87 18.98 -1.57 18.11 -1.57 C 17.24 -1.57 16.54 -0.87 16.54 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}$}{\kern 0.86108pt}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{$\scriptstyle\psi$}\big).\end{array}

Thus, we see that the VFE update, as minimiser of the left-hand-side
in (
34
) — see
Proposition
2
(
2
) — is
used to approximate the Jeffrey update
\omega{\kern-0.86108pt}\raisebox{-5.16663pt}{$\scalebox{0.9}{\hbox to2.68pt{\vbox to12.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip-11.75017pt\lower-1.3381pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 19.69 0 L 19.69 14.76}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}{{{{}{}{}{}}}{{}{}{}{}}}{}
{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 19.69 0 C 19.69 -0.87 18.98 -1.57 18.11 -1.57 C 17.24 -1.57 16.54 -0.87 16.54 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}$}{\kern 0.86108pt}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{$\scriptstyle\psi$}
, on the
right-hand-side in (
34
), via
KL-divergence. This VFE update may approximate the Jeffrey update, but
it does not do a crucial thing that Jeffrey update does, namely
correct errors — see below — in the form of decrease of
KL-divergence. This is remarkable, since predictive coding is all
about error correction.
